{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Design research for a portfolio's CASE-STUDY/EVIDENCE pages (July 2026). Context: read /Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0/docs/design-lab/DOSSIER-SPEC.md, EVIDENCE-MODEL.md, DECISION.md, and NO-LIST.md first (mandatory). The site is a warm-paper \"working paper on trust & machinery\" (Fraunces/Newsreader/Fragment Mono, ink + clay accent, hand-drawn thread, stamps, folios); its case-study pages are being rebuilt as archive-cream DOSSIERS with receipts tables, tipped-in plates, eval-protocol blocks, and a rendered proof manifest (/evidence index).\n\nUse web search/fetch to research HOW THE BEST present technical case studies, documentation, and evidence:\n1. Award-tier case-study pages 2025–26 (Awwwards SOTD portfolios' project pages, not their heroes): structure, meta extraction, figure/caption conventions, prev/next continuity. 2–3 standouts with URLs.\n2. Research-paper / academic-styled web design done well (arXiv-adjacent aesthetics, distill.pub legacy, modern \"paper-styled\" sites/blogs): apparatus worth stealing (figure numbering, tables, footnotes/sidenotes — e.g. Tufte-css sidenotes, gwern.net, typography of evidence tables).\n3. Evidence/provenance presentation in industry: how eval reports, model cards (HuggingFace), benchmark pages (MLPerf), and changelogs present verifiable numbers (dates, methods, reproduce commands, commit pins) in readable form. What transfers to a personal dossier?\n4. \"Documentation as artifact\" patterns: downloadable JSON/CSV ledgers, file@commit deep links, CI-run badges/links done tastefully (NOT badge-wall clutter — banned vibe).\n5. Visit-memory / reading-history patterns (the \"paper remembers\" elevation): sites that mark visited/read state elegantly.\n\nOutput: markdown report — (a) 4–6 exemplar links each with the ONE transferable idea, (b) apparatus recommendations mapped onto the 8-point dossier spec (confirm/refine each point with a precedent), (c) 5 concrete \"steal this\" details for evidence tables/figure plates/protocol blocks, (d) any warning where the dossier spec risks cliché or NO-LIST adjacency. Raw markdown only, cite URLs."},"uuid":"4114af81-edcd-46f5-8678-088c9de77553","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:07:53.401Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

{"parentUuid":"34dd12fe-d356-4666-993f-658bec547c8a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- cheat: Print the full Claude Code cheatsheet — slash commands, hooks, statusline, CLIs\n- clis: Interactive picker — choose a CLI to see what it does and how to use it\n- menu: Interactive picker — toggle every Claude Code feature on/off\n- toggles: Show current state of every feature toggle (read-only)\n- vercel:bootstrap: Bootstrap a repository with Vercel-linked resources by running preflight checks, provisioning integrations, verifying env keys, and then executing db/dev startup commands safely.\n- vercel:deploy: Deploy the current project to Vercel. Pass \"prod\" or \"production\" as argument to deploy to production. Default is preview deployment.\n- vercel:env: Manage Vercel environment variables. Commands include list, pull, add, remove, and diff. Use to sync environment variables between Vercel and your local development environment.\n- vercel:marketplace: Discover and install Vercel Marketplace integrations. Use to find databases, CMS, auth providers, and other services available on the Vercel Marketplace.\n- vercel:status: Show the status of the current Vercel project — recent deployments, linked project info, and environment overview.\n- pdf-viewer:annotate: Collaboratively annotate a PDF — propose markup, review together, iterate\n- pdf-viewer:fill-form: Fill PDF form fields interactively with live visual feedback\n- pdf-viewer:open: Open a PDF in the interactive viewer\n- pdf-viewer:sign: Place a signature or initials image on a PDF\n- vercel:ai-gateway: Vercel AI Gateway expert guidance. Use when configuring model routing, provider failover, cost tracking, or managing multiple AI providers through a unified API.\n- vercel:ai-sdk: Vercel AI SDK expert guidance. Use when building AI-powered features — chat interfaces, text generation, structured output, tool calling, agents, MCP integration, streaming, embeddings, reranking, image generation, or working with any LLM provider.\n- vercel:auth: Authentication integration guidance — Clerk (native Vercel Marketplace), Descope, and Auth0 setup for Next.js applications. Covers middleware auth patterns, sign-in/sign-up flows, and Marketplace provisioning. Use when implementing user authentication.\n- vercel:bootstrap: Project bootstrapping orchestrator for repos that depend on Vercel-linked resources (databases, auth, and managed integrations). Use when setting up or repairing a repository so linking, environment provisioning, env pulls, and first-run db/dev commands happen in the correct safe order.\n- vercel:chat-sdk: Vercel Chat SDK expert guidance. Use when building multi-platform chat bots — Slack, Telegram, Microsoft Teams, Discord, Google Chat, GitHub, Linear — with a single codebase. Covers the Chat class, adapters, threads, messages, cards, modals, streaming, state management, and webhook setup.\n- vercel:deployments-cicd: Vercel deployment and CI/CD expert guidance. Use when deploying, promoting, rolling back, inspecting deployments, building with --prebuilt, or configuring CI workflow files for Vercel.\n- vercel:env-vars: Vercel environment variable expert guidance. Use when working with .env files, vercel env commands, OIDC tokens, or managing environment-specific configuration.\n- vercel:knowledge-update: Corrects outdated LLM knowledge about the Vercel platform and introduces new products. Injected at session start.\n- vercel:marketplace: Vercel Marketplace expert guidance — discovering, installing, and building integrations, auto-provisioned environment variables, unified billing, and the vercel integration CLI. Use when consuming third-party services, building custom integrations, or managing marketplace resources on Vercel.\n- vercel:next-cache-components: Next.js 16 Cache Components guidance — PPR, use cache directive, cacheLife, cacheTag, updateTag, and migration from unstable_cache. Use when implementing partial prerendering, caching strategies, or migrating from older Next.js cache patterns.\n- vercel:next-forge: next-forge expert guidance — production-grade Turborepo monorepo SaaS starter by Vercel. Use when working in a next-forge project, scaffolding with `npx next-forge init`, or editing @repo/* workspace packages.\n- vercel:next-upgrade: Upgrade Next.js to the latest version following official migration guides and codemods. Use when upgrading Next.js versions, running codemods, or migrating between major releases.\n- vercel:nextjs: Next.js App Router expert guidance. Use when building, debugging, or architecting Next.js applications — routing, Server Components, Server Actions, Cache Components, layouts, middleware/proxy, data fetching, rendering strategies, and deployment on Vercel.\n- vercel:react-best-practices: React best-practices reviewer for TSX files. Triggers after editing multiple TSX components to run a condensed quality checklist covering component structure, hooks usage, accessibility, performance, and TypeScript patterns.\n- vercel:routing-middleware: Vercel Routing Middleware guidance — request interception before cache, rewrites, redirects, personalization. Works with any framework. Supports Edge, Node.js, and Bun runtimes. Use when intercepting requests at the platform level.\n- vercel:runtime-cache: Vercel Runtime Cache API guidance — ephemeral per-region key-value cache with tag-based invalidation. Shared across Functions, Routing Middleware, and Builds. Use when implementing caching strategies beyond framework-level caching.\n- vercel:shadcn: shadcn/ui expert guidance — CLI, component installation, composition patterns, custom registries, theming, Tailwind CSS integration, and high-quality interface design. Use when initializing shadcn, adding components, composing product UI, building custom registries, configuring themes, or troubleshooting component issues.\n- vercel:turbopack: Turbopack expert guidance. Use when configuring the Next.js bundler, optimizing HMR, debugging build issues, or understanding the Turbopack vs Webpack differences.\n- vercel:vercel-agent: Vercel Agent guidance — AI-powered code review, incident investigation, and SDK installation. Automates PR analysis and anomaly debugging. Use when configuring or understanding Vercel's AI development tools.\n- vercel:vercel-cli: Vercel CLI expert guidance. Use when deploying, managing environment variables, linking projects, viewing logs, managing domains, or interacting with the Vercel platform from the command line.\n- vercel:vercel-functions: Vercel Functions expert guidance — Serverless Functions, Edge Functions, Fluid Compute, streaming, Cron Jobs, and runtime configuration. Use when configuring, debugging, or optimizing server-side code running on Vercel.\n- vercel:vercel-sandbox: Vercel Sandbox guidance — ephemeral Firecracker microVMs for running untrusted code safely. Supports AI agents, code generation, and experimentation. Use when executing user-generated or AI-generated code in isolation.\n- vercel:vercel-storage: Vercel storage expert guidance — Blob, Edge Config, and Marketplace storage (Neon Postgres, Upstash Redis). Use when choosing, configuring, or using data storage with Vercel applications.\n- vercel:verification: Full-story verification — infers what the user is building, then verifies the complete flow end-to-end: browser → API → data → response. Triggers on dev server start and 'why isn't this working' signals.\n- vercel:workflow: Vercel Workflow DevKit (WDK) expert guidance. Use when building durable workflows, long-running tasks, API routes or agents that need pause/resume, retries, step-based execution, or crash-safe orchestration with Vercel Workflow.\n- pdf-viewer:view-pdf: Interactive PDF viewer. Use when the user wants to open, show, or view a PDF and collaborate on it visually — annotate, highlight, stamp, fill form fields, place signature/initials, or review markup together. Not for summarization or text extraction (use native Read instead).\n- legal:brief: Generate contextual briefings for legal work — daily summary, topic research, or incident response. Use when starting your day and need a scan of legal-relevant items across email, calendar, and contracts, when researching a specific legal question across internal sources, or when a developing situation (data breach, litigation threat, regulatory inquiry) needs rapid context.\n- legal:compliance-check: Run a compliance check on a proposed action, product feature, or business initiative, surfacing applicable regulations, required approvals, and risk areas. Use when launching a feature that touches personal data, when marketing or product proposes something with regulatory implications, or when you need to know which approvals and jurisdictional requirements apply before proceeding.\n- legal:legal-response: Generate a response to a common legal inquiry using configured templates, with built-in escalation checks for situations that shouldn't use a templated reply. Use when responding to data subject requests, litigation hold notices, vendor legal questions, NDA requests from business teams, or subpoenas.\n- legal:legal-risk-assessment: Assess and classify legal risks using a severity-by-likelihood framework with escalation criteria. Use when evaluating contract risk, assessing deal exposure, classifying issues by severity, or determining whether a matter needs senior counsel or outside legal review.\n- legal:meeting-briefing: Prepare structured briefings for meetings with legal relevance and track resulting action items. Use when preparing for contract negotiations, board meetings, compliance reviews, or any meeting where legal context, background research, or action tracking is needed.\n- legal:review-contract: Review a contract against your organization's negotiation playbook — flag deviations, generate redlines, provide business impact analysis. Use when reviewing vendor or customer agreements, when you need clause-by-clause analysis against standard positions, or when preparing a negotiation strategy with prioritized redlines and fallback positions.\n- legal:signature-request: Prepare and route a document for e-signature — run a pre-signature checklist, configure signing order, and send for execution. Use when a contract is finalized and ready to sign, when verifying entity names, exhibits, and signature blocks before sending, or when setting up an envelope with sequential or parallel signers.\n- legal:triage-nda: Rapidly triage an incoming NDA and classify it as GREEN (standard approval), YELLOW (counsel review), or RED (full legal review). Use when a new NDA arrives from sales or business development, when screening for embedded non-solicits, non-competes, or missing carveouts, or when deciding whether an NDA can be signed under standard delegation.\n- legal:vendor-check: Check the status of existing agreements with a vendor across all connected systems — CLM, CRM, email, and document storage — with gap analysis and upcoming deadlines. Use when onboarding or renewing a vendor, when you need a consolidated view of what's signed and what's missing (MSA, DPA, SOW), or when checking for approaching expirations and surviving obligations.\n- productivity:memory-management: Two-tier memory system that makes Claude a true workplace collaborator. Decodes shorthand, acronyms, nicknames, and internal language so Claude understands requests like a colleague would. CLAUDE.md for working memory, memory/ directory for the full knowledge base.\n- productivity:start: Initialize the productivity system and open the dashboard. Use when setting up the plugin for the first time, bootstrapping working memory from your existing task list, or decoding the shorthand (nicknames, acronyms, project codenames) you use in your todos.\n- productivity:task-management: Simple task management using a shared TASKS.md file. Reference this when the user asks about their tasks, wants to add/complete tasks, or needs help tracking commitments.\n- productivity:update: Sync tasks and refresh memory from your current activity. Use when pulling new assignments from your project tracker into TASKS.md, triaging stale or overdue tasks, filling memory gaps for unknown people or projects, or running a comprehensive scan to catch todos buried in chat and email.\n- cowork-plugin-management:cowork-plugin-customizer: Customize a Claude Code plugin for a specific organization's tools and workflows. Use when: customize plugin, set up plugin, configure plugin, tailor plugin, adjust plugin settings, customize plugin connectors, customize plugin skill, tweak plugin, modify plugin configuration.\n- cowork-plugin-management:create-cowork-plugin: Guide users through creating a new plugin from scratch in a cowork session. Use when users want to create a plugin, build a plugin, make a new plugin, develop a plugin, scaffold a plugin, start a plugin from scratch, or design a plugin. This skill requires Cowork mode with access to the outputs directory for delivering the final .plugin file.\n- marketing:brand-review: Review content against your brand voice, style guide, and messaging pillars, flagging deviations by severity with specific before/after fixes. Use when checking a draft before it ships, when auditing copy for voice consistency and terminology, or when screening for unsubstantiated claims, missing disclaimers, and other legal flags.\n- marketing:campaign-plan: Generate a full campaign brief with objectives, audience, messaging, channel strategy, content calendar, and success metrics. Use when planning a product launch, lead-gen push, or awareness campaign, when you need a week-by-week content calendar with dependencies, or when translating a marketing goal into a structured, executable plan.\n- marketing:competitive-brief: Research competitors and generate a positioning and messaging comparison with content gaps, opportunities, and threats. Use when building sales battlecards, when finding positioning gaps and messaging angles competitors haven't claimed, or when a competitor makes a move and you need to assess the impact.\n- marketing:content-creation: Draft marketing content across channels — blog posts, social media, email newsletters, landing pages, press releases, and case studies. Use when writing any marketing content, when you need channel-specific formatting, SEO-optimized copy, headline options, or calls to action.\n- marketing:draft-content: Draft blog posts, social media, email newsletters, landing pages, press releases, and case studies with channel-specific formatting and SEO recommendations. Use when writing any marketing content, when you need headline or subject line options, or when adapting a message for a specific platform, audience, and brand voice.\n- marketing:email-sequence: Design and draft multi-email sequences with full copy, timing, branching logic, exit conditions, and performance benchmarks. Use when building onboarding, lead nurture, re-engagement, win-back, or product launch flows, when you need a complete drip campaign with A/B test suggestions, or when mapping a sequence end-to-end with a flow diagram.\n- marketing:performance-report: Build a marketing performance report with key metrics, trend analysis, wins and misses, and prioritized optimization recommendations. Use when wrapping a campaign, when preparing weekly, monthly, or quarterly channel summaries for stakeholders, or when you need data translated into an executive summary with next-period priorities.\n- marketing:seo-audit: Run a comprehensive SEO audit — keyword research, on-page analysis, content gaps, technical checks, and competitor comparison. Use when assessing a site's SEO health, when finding keyword opportunities and content gaps competitors own, or when you need a prioritized action plan split into quick wins and strategic investments.\n- engineering:architecture: Create or evaluate an architecture decision record (ADR). Use when choosing between technologies (e.g., Kafka vs SQS), documenting a design decision with trade-offs and consequences, reviewing a system design proposal, or designing a new component from requirements and constraints.\n- engineering:code-review: Review code changes for security, performance, and correctness. Trigger with a PR URL or diff, \"review this before I merge\", \"is this code safe?\", or when checking a change for N+1 queries, injection risks, missing edge cases, or error handling gaps.\n- engineering:debug: Structured debugging session — reproduce, isolate, diagnose, and fix. Trigger with an error message or stack trace, \"this works in staging but not prod\", \"something broke after the deploy\", or when behavior diverges from expected and the cause isn't obvious.\n- engineering:deploy-checklist: Pre-deployment verification checklist. Use when about to ship a release, deploying a change with database migrations or feature flags, verifying CI status and approvals before going to production, or documenting rollback triggers ahead of time.\n- engineering:documentation: Write and maintain technical documentation. Trigger with \"write docs for\", \"document this\", \"create a README\", \"write a runbook\", \"onboarding guide\", or when the user needs help with any form of technical writing — API docs, architecture docs, or operational runbooks.\n- engineering:incident-response: Run an incident response workflow — triage, communicate, and write postmortem. Trigger with \"we have an incident\", \"production is down\", an alert that needs severity assessment, a status update mid-incident, or when writing a blameless postmortem after resolution.\n- engineering:standup: Generate a standup update from recent activity. Use when preparing for daily standup, summarizing yesterday's commits and PRs and ticket moves, formatting work into yesterday/today/blockers, or structuring a few rough notes into a shareable update.\n- engineering:system-design: Design systems, services, and architectures. Trigger with \"design a system for\", \"how should we architect\", \"system design for\", \"what's the right architecture for\", or when the user needs help with API design, data modeling, or service boundaries.\n- engineering:tech-debt: Identify, categorize, and prioritize technical debt. Trigger with \"tech debt\", \"technical debt audit\", \"what should we refactor\", \"code health\", or when the user asks about code quality, refactoring priorities, or maintenance backlog.\n- engineering:testing-strategy: Design test strategies and test plans. Trigger with \"how should we test\", \"test strategy for\", \"write tests for\", \"test plan\", \"what tests do we need\", or when the user needs help with testing approaches, coverage, or test architecture.\n- data:analyze: Answer data questions -- from quick lookups to full analyses. Use when looking up a single metric, investigating what's driving a trend or drop, comparing segments over time, or preparing a formal data report for stakeholders.\n- data:build-dashboard: Build an interactive HTML dashboard with charts, filters, and tables. Use when creating an executive overview with KPI cards, turning query results into a shareable self-contained report, building a team monitoring snapshot, or needing multiple charts with filters in one browser-openable file.\n- data:create-viz: Create publication-quality visualizations with Python. Use when turning query results or a DataFrame into a chart, selecting the right chart type for a trend or comparison, generating a plot for a report or presentation, or needing an interactive chart with hover and zoom.\n- data:data-context-extractor: Generate or improve a company-specific data analysis skill by extracting tribal knowledge from analysts.\nBOOTSTRAP MODE - Triggers: \"Create a data context skill\", \"Set up data analysis for our warehouse\", \"Help me create a skill for our database\", \"Generate a data skill for [company]\" → Discovers schemas, asks key questions, generates initial skill with reference files\nITERATION MODE - Triggers: \"Add context about [domain]\", \"The skill needs more info about [topic]\", \"Update the data skill with [metrics/tables/terminology]\", \"Improve the [domain] reference\" → Loads existing skill, asks targeted questions, appends/updates reference files\nUse when data analysts want Claude to understand their company's specific data warehouse, terminology, metrics definitions, and common query patterns.\n- data:data-visualization: Create effective data visualizations with Python (matplotlib, seaborn, plotly). Use when building charts, choosing the right chart type for a dataset, creating publication-quality figures, or applying design principles like accessibility and color theory.\n- data:explore-data: Profile and explore a dataset to understand its shape, quality, and patterns. Use when encountering a new table or file, checking null rates and column distributions, spotting data quality issues like duplicates or suspicious values, or deciding which dimensions and metrics to analyze.\n- data:sql-queries: Write correct, performant SQL across all major data warehouse dialects (Snowflake, BigQuery, Databricks, PostgreSQL, etc.). Use when writing queries, optimizing slow SQL, translating between dialects, or building complex analytical queries with CTEs, window functions, or aggregations.\n- data:statistical-analysis: Apply statistical methods including descriptive stats, trend analysis, outlier detection, and hypothesis testing. Use when analyzing distributions, testing for significance, detecting anomalies, computing correlations, or interpreting statistical results.\n- data:validate-data: QA an analysis before sharing -- methodology, accuracy, and bias checks. Use when reviewing an analysis before a stakeholder presentation, spot-checking calculations and aggregation logic, verifying a SQL query's results look right, or assessing whether conclusions are actually supported by the data.\n- data:write-query: Write optimized SQL for your dialect with best practices. Use when translating a natural-language data need into SQL, building a multi-CTE query with joins and aggregations, optimizing a query against a large partitioned table, or getting dialect-specific syntax for Snowflake, BigQuery, Postgres, etc.\n- design:accessibility-review: Run a WCAG 2.1 AA accessibility audit on a design or page. Trigger with \"audit accessibility\", \"check a11y\", \"is this accessible?\", or when reviewing a design for color contrast, keyboard navigation, touch target size, or screen reader behavior before handoff.\n- design:design-critique: Get structured design feedback on usability, hierarchy, and consistency. Trigger with \"review this design\", \"critique this mockup\", \"what do you think of this screen?\", or when sharing a Figma link or screenshot for feedback at any stage from exploration to final polish.\n- design:design-handoff: Generate developer handoff specs from a design. Use when a design is ready for engineering and needs a spec sheet covering layout, design tokens, component props, interaction states, responsive breakpoints, edge cases, and animation details.\n- design:design-system: Audit, document, or extend your design system. Use when checking for naming inconsistencies or hardcoded values across components, writing documentation for a component's variants, states, and accessibility notes, or designing a new pattern that fits the existing system.\n- design:research-synthesis: Synthesize user research into themes, insights, and recommendations. Use when you have interview transcripts, survey results, usability test notes, support tickets, or NPS responses that need to be distilled into patterns, user segments, and prioritized next steps.\n- design:user-research\n- design:ux-copy\n- anthropic-skills:canvas-design\n- anthropic-skills:consolidate-memory: Reflective pass over your memory files — merge duplicates, fix stale facts, prune the index.\n- anthropic-skills:docx\n- anthropic-skills:mcp-builder\n- anthropic-skills:morning\n- anthropic-skills:pdf\n- anthropic-skills:pptx\n- anthropic-skills:schedule\n- anthropic-skills:setup-cowork: Guided Cowork setup — install role-matched plugins, connect your tools, try a skill.\n- anthropic-skills:skill-creator\n- anthropic-skills:theme-factory\n- anthropic-skills:web-artifacts-builder\n- anthropic-skills:xlsx\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- artifact-design: Design guidance and fundamentals for Artifacts.\n- artifact-capabilities: Runtime capabilities a published Artifact can declare — calling the user's claude.ai connectors (MCP) from the page, and future abilities. Load this BEFORE passing `capabilities` to the Artifact tool or writing any `window.claude.mcp` code.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- {"parentUuid":"521cedfd-9fe3-4cf3-9cd7-28ce7877e4c3","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JEsSdw9QxKGcCq6VCu2Eso","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# The case file as working-paper dossier (visitor journey critique, 2026-07-18)\n2\t\n3\tJourney score 54/100: home ~85 · case pages ~55 · THE TRANSITION ~20.\n4\t\"Two unrelated design systems joined by a shared header… the case page's own\n5\teyebrow says EVIDENCE LEDGER — the concept is already the home's language,\n6\twearing another site's clothes.\" Rebuild = the missing half of the concept.\n7\t\n8\t## The 8-point dossier spec (adopt)\n9\t1. STOCK: same paper one stop older — flat \"archive\" cream (NO day-arc; a\n10\t   document has fixed daylight), contour texture retained; ink + stamp-orange\n11\t   as the only accent.\n12\t2. OPENING: ¶ kicker \"case file 02 / 07 · agentic automl — filed 2026-02\",\n13\t   Fraunces title, Newsreader deck; thread enters top edge, underlines title,\n14\t   runs the left margin throughout.\n15\t3. META AS LEDGER: role/timeframe/stack/repo as mono dot-leader table\n16\t   (hairline rules, no pills/cards).\n17\t4. EVIDENCE AS PLATES: screenshots = tipped-in figures (thin ink frame, paper\n18\t   mat, mono caption \"fig. 1 — …, run no. 041\", source line → repo/commit);\n19\t   architecture = inked fig. 2 (ink arrows, not green).\n20\t5. VALIDATION AS APPENDIX TABLE (the centerpiece): claim | probe | result |\n21\t   date, mono + hairline rules, inked ✓ per verified row — merge with the\n22\t   EVIDENCE-MODEL receipts columns (artifact file@SHA + visibility badge).\n23\t6. DECISIONS AS NUMBERED CLAUSES: \"d1 — …\", serif rationale, tradeoff as\n24\t   indented mono footnote. No cards.\n25\t7. STAMPS FOR STATUS: private-proof disclaimer = dashed orange stamp\n26\t   (\"private repository — evidence verified\"); outcomes bear small approved\n27\t   marks echoing the gate.\n28\t8. CLOSE WITH CONTINUITY: folio footer \"case file 2 of 7 — return to the\n29\t   paper ⟵ / next file: jobtracker ⟶\"; thread exits toward the next document.\n30\t\n31\t## Elevations (both adopted into backlog)\n32\t- TIPPED-IN PLATES: every screenshot a photographic plate (mat, ±0.4°\n33\t  rotation, thread stitching a corner, caption + commit hash) — dark\n34\t  screenshots become photographs OF a dark screen on light paper.\n35\t- THE PAPER REMEMBERS: one-time \"opened · jul 18, 10:04 am local\" stamp per\n36\t  case file; home rail + work rows ink a ✓ for visited files — the reader's\n37\t  journey becomes the audit trail.\n38\t\n39\t## Homepage fixes found en route (fold into build round)\n40\t- MOBILE THREAD COLLIDES WITH TEXT at 390 (ch 03–05: line sits on\n41\t  letterforms) — reserve a true 20–24px gutter ≤480px or run at screen edge.\n42\t- Illegible header at case-page landing (dark ink over #090b0d at scroll-0)\n43\t  — header must read the surface beneath; interim: force dark variant on\n44\t  case routes.\n45\t- Case-page color anarchy (5 accents) → ink + stamp-orange only.\n46\t- Viewport sag: two dead-frame moments (hero byline→flagship gap; mid-chapter\n47\t  blank halves) — tighten min-heights so apparatus always enters the frame.\n48\t\n49\t## Recruiter journey addendum (68/100 — unique finds)\n50\t- FLAGSHIP HAS ZERO EVIDENCE LINKS (links-automl.json: empty) — no artifacts\n51\t  section renders on /projects/automl/ at all. {"parentUuid":"778ece65-2336-443c-b4a8-61472c3f038a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0193zBvxck58kDhov48vzjq5","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Design-direction decision — July 17, 2026\n2\t\n3\tThree candidates built by parallel agents, shot at 1440/390 (hero/mid/end/mobile),\n4\tjudged against: striking-ness, NO-LIST, identity fit, buildability, and the\n5\t2025–26 award formula (two colors, one big idea, motion as the brand).\n6\t\n7\t## Scores (visual judgment, all 12 frames reviewed)\n8\t\n9\t| | Candidate | Hero | Depth | Ending | Verdict |\n10\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n11\t| A | Daylight Field Notes | 7.5 | 7 | 7.5 | Faithful to plan but SAFE — timid hero scale, light-rake barely reads, thread is edge decoration not protagonist |\n12\t| B | Warm Ledger | 8 | 7.5 | 8 | Handsomest surprise; true lamplight warmth, great micro-copy (\"runs without sign-off → none. ever.\"). BUT: dark = the portfolio crowd; card-gravity visible at mid-scroll; forfeits recruiter-light readability + the day-arc |\n13\t| C | Ink & Paper | 9 | 9 | 9.5 | The only one that stops you. Masthead-scale Fraunces, working-paper apparatus, ONE continuous hand-drawn line that becomes fig.1 (draft→train→evaluate→human gate) and terminates at the page's only color: the clay APPROVED stamp |\n14\t\n15\t## CHOSEN: \"The Working Paper\" — C's skeleton in A's living world, B's dusk\n16\t\n17\tC wins the comparison but forfeits our biggest atmospheric weapon (the day-arc).\n18\tThe unified direction:\n19\t\n20\t1. **Layout DNA from C**: masthead-scale hero (~10rem Fraunces, 0.95 leading),\n21\t   working-paper frame (\"a working paper on trust & machinery\"), print apparatus\n22\t   (¶ kickers, folios NN/07, datelines, fig. captions, footnote that resolves the\n23\t   hero's superscript, colophon), vast whitespace, no cards/surfaces on day pages.\n24\t2. **The line from C** (= the plan's Red Thread, grammar proven): ONE continuous\n25\t   hand-wobbled INK line travels the whole page — underlines the hero accent,\n26\t   becomes each chapter's hand-drawn diagram, crosses every folio rule.\n27\t   Ink during day; passed nodes tick PINE; **clay is reserved for decisions** —\n28\t   gate moments and the final distressed APPROVED stamp (C's discipline).\n29\t3. **The world from A/plan**: day-arc background travel dawn→dusk (oklch,\n30\t   scroll-scrubbed), ≤5% grain, raking light made PERCEPTIBLE (Daylight-style),\n31\t   full type system (Fraunces opsz/SOFT + Newsreader + Fragment Mono lowercase).\n32\t4. **Dusk from B**: after the Ch-06 flip the paper becomes lamplight ledger\n33\t   (waypoint-06/07 tokens already encode B's browns) — B's surface language\n34\t   makes the flip MEANINGFUL: the working paper's day ends, the ledger closes.\n35\t5. **Wit in the apparatus**, never effects: footnotes, \"still in review\" honesty,\n36\t   colophon (\"set by hand · two inks. one line. seven chapters.\").\n37\t\n38\tNO-LIST status: all three candidates audited clean by their builders (trailing\n39\tHTML comments); the synthesis inherits only audited elements.\n40\t\n41\tMaster-plan status: concept VALIDATED by competition — thread/day-arc/gate all\n42\tsurvive; what changes is execution grammar: bigger type, print apparatus,\n43\thand-drawn line, one-clay discipline, working-paper voice.\n44\t\n45\tFiles: candidates/*.html · shots/*.png (12) · see INSPIRATION.md for sources.\n46\t"}]},"uuid":"904768d7-2221-4474-a363-6a1b556c3961","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:07:57.653Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"778ece65-2336-443c-b4a8-61472c3f038a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

{"parentUuid":"1e3c334d-9d50-40c2-bde1-71c347f8fbd6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01J7prUnaEA66T4snoVYPbVh","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Design-lab inspiration inventory (Chrome tour, July 2026)\n2\t\n3\t## Observed live (screenshots taken, real Chrome)\n4\t\n5\t### daylightcomputer.com — the warmth benchmark\n6\t- Warmth comes from LIGHT IN IMAGERY, not flat cream: hero is golden-hour\n7\t  photography; the paper sections carry soft diagonal LIGHT RAYS raking across\n8\t  the background. A flat `#FAF6EF` div is NOT the move — light must feel real.\n9\t- Scroll-scrubbed word-by-word ink reveal (\"Introducing *Daylight* — …\") with\n10\t  ONE italic serif accent word — exactly our Ch-02 manifesto spec, proven live.\n11\t- Editorial serif headline over photography; tiny tracked-caps labels.\n12\t- **Steal:** light-ray treatment for our Layer 0 (sun as raking light, not\n13\t  radial gradient); the word-scrub cadence.\n14\t\n15\t### robin-noguier.com\n16\t- Per-project full-viewport COLOR-WORLD takeover (slate panel slides over\n17\t  near-black carrying the case study). Discrete slides; ours (continuous\n18\t  day-arc) is the more original evolution of the same instinct.\n19\t- Giant display-serif name in muted slate blue on near-black; personality\n20\t  micro-copy (fake \"dark patterns\" toast) — wit humanizes.\n21\t- Velocity-reactive image warp/skew on scroll — memorable but floaty; SKIP\n22\t  (banned-adjacent).\n23\t- Right-edge dot rail for wayfinding.\n24\t- **Steal:** color-world-per-chapter conviction; micro-copy wit.\n25\t\n26\t### press.stripe.com\n27\t- ONE physical metaphor executed completely: books as literal 3D spines in a\n28\t  stack; scroll separates the stack; each spine textured, colored, dimensional.\n29\t  Warm near-black canvas + rich spine palette.\n30\t- Left-edge minimal tick rail appears on scroll (quiet wayfinding).\n31\t- Nothing on the page is generic: no cards, no grids — the METAPHOR is the UI.\n32\t- **Steal:** commit to our pipeline/approval-gate metaphor as literally as\n33\t  Stripe commits to books. The red thread must BE the interface, not decor.\n34\t\n35\t### dennissnellenberg.com\n36\t- Multilingual greeting preloader (Hello → स्वागत हे → Ciao). Famous, now\n37\t  widely copied — treat as cliché, do NOT copy. (Also violates our \"no boot\n38\t  screens\" spirit.) Site works fine in Chrome; didn't tour past preloader.\n39\t\n40\t## Principles extracted (for the candidate mockups)\n41\t1. Warmth = light, not hex. Simulated raking light/grain beats flat cream.\n42\t2. One metaphor, total commitment (Stripe books ⇒ our pipeline-run + gate).\n43\t3. Color-world conviction per chapter — but continuous (day-arc), not slides.\n44\t4. Personality through micro-copy wit, not effects.\n45\t5. Editorial serif + tiny tracked mono labels is the confirmed voice.\n46\t6. Quiet edge rail wayfinding (ticks/numbers), appearing after the hero.\n47\t\n48\t### niccolomiranda.com — \"The Paper Portfolio\" (toured live)\n49\t- Full newspaper metaphor: masthead, dateline, column rules, drop caps, boxed\n50\t  dashed-border testimonial \"clippings\", giant condensed display type.\n51\t- Mid-scroll the entire sheet ROTATES like paper turned on a desk.\n52\t- A literal POSTAGE STAMP graphic (orange sunburst rays + signature +\n53\t  name/date fields) — validates our approval-stamp motif.\n54\t- Transpose (don't copy): newspaper→ ML run-log/research dossier; stamps =\n55\t  approval gates; clay sunburst = our day-arc sun.\n56\t\n57\t### Research digest (agent, July 2026)\n58\t- 2025–26 winner formula: TWO colors, ONE big idea, motion as the brand\n59\t  (Pacôme #0a0a0a/#fafafa, Elliott, Olha). Maximalist WebGL is now the outlier.\n60\t- MindMarket (SOTM Dec 2025): drawn line leads the narrative = our red thread,\n61\t  award-validated (Rive + GSAP).\n62\t- Toolset verdicts: SplitText 2025 rewrite (a11y-safe masked line reveals),\n63\t  DrawSVG for the thread/diagrams, Flip for list↔grid morph (Pacôme move),\n64\t  CSS cross-document View Transitions for index→case-study continuity\n65\t  (Chrome/Safari, degrades in Firefox), ONE variable-wght kinetic headline\n66\t  axis. Motion/anime.js skipped (second engine). Rive = consider later.\n67\t- Ten stealable moves logged; watch-out: keep any high-chroma accent on warm\n68\t  paper, never near-black (Lando lime = one hex from banned console look).\n69\t"}]},"uuid":"5fdbcd76-c7ef-4c75-ad10-407830b551f8","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:08:11.471Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"1e3c334d-9d50-40c2-bde1-71c347f8fbd6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

Here are the key insights from award-winning portfolio websites on Awwwards:\n\n## Portfolio Page Structure\n\nA portfolio showcases the projects carried out by a company and is the most important content on design agency or architecture websites.\n\n## Effective Case Study Page Structures\n\nBased on award-winning examples, here are some best practices:\n\n**Simple, Content-Focused Approach:**\nEffective portfolio sites typically consist of 4 main pages: a homepage with featured projects and contact details, an About page detailing career history and skillsets, a project archive, and a project detail page.\n\n**Minimalist Design Principles:**\nLarge headings, abundant white space, and conciseness in element arrangement characterize successful portfolios, often using typography as a primary visual element rather than heavy graphic content.\n\n**User Experience Enhancements:**\nA minimal design requires an immersive experience, brought to life with micro-interactions, dynamic screen and page transitions, and strong use of video.\n\n**Strategic Content Structure:**\nTop designers structure case studies to highlight ROI and user-centric solutions, focusing on specific tactics and approaches. Selecting 3-5 core case studies that align with your goal, where each tells a complete story including the challenge, unique process, specific contribution, and measurable outcome.\n\nThese best practices emphasize clarity, meaningful interactions, and clear navigation over over-complicated animations.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"71e694e8-65b5-4907-9f03-0e99284dcd47","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:08:59.218Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c3de0105-46ac-4e85-b0c7-bf2f39c9910a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

Systematic Analysis of 32K AI Model Cards\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.05160\"},{\"title\":\"Annotated Model Card Template · Hugging Face\",\"url\":\"https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/model-card-annotated\"},{\"title\":\"Hugging Face Model Card - GeeksforGeeks\",\"url\":\"https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/data-science/hugging-face-model-card/\"},{\"title\":\"AI Transparency Atlas: Framework, Scoring, and Real-Time Model Card Evaluation Pipeline\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2512.12443\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, here are the key findings about Hugging Face model card evaluation results presentation best practices:\n\n## Evaluation Section Structure\n\nThe evaluation section describes the evaluation protocols, what is being measured in the evaluation, and provides the results. Evaluation ideally has at least two parts, with one part looking at quantitative measurement of general performance (Testing Data, Factors & Metrics), such as may be done with benchmarking; and another looking at performance with respect to specific social safety issues (Societal Impact Assessment), such as may be done with red-teaming.\n\n## Structured Results Presentation\n\nYou can specify your model's evaluation results in a structured way in the model card metadata, and results are parsed by the Hub and displayed in a widget on the model page.\n\n## Key Documentation Categories\n\nRecent analysis of Hugging Face model cards shows that over 90% of model cards included Model Architecture, Evaluation Metrics, and Compute Requirements. However, Safety Evaluation (approximately 65%) and Bias and Fairness (approximately 60%) were often addressed superficially.\n\n## Best Practice Recommendations\n\nEvaluation is ideally disaggregated with respect to different factors, such as task, domain and population subgroup; and calculated with metrics that are most meaningful for foreseeable contexts of use. Additionally, it's recommended to read Hugging Face metric cards (documentation) for detailed explanations, limitations, and appropriate use cases.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"fa56cae5-f676-4904-911f-5e011e5769b2","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:00.218Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ef43bbac-5528-405d-bc46-618baead2450","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"6a9b0ce3-1b93-477b-809b-abc5b544739b","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014KH5XCejLkd6C1ShPabEiy","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"Epoch AI benchmarking hub methodology downloadable data transparency\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Introducing Epoch AI's AI benchmarking hub | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/blog/introducing-benchmarks-dashboard\"},{\"title\":\"A more systematic and transparent AI benchmarking hub | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/blog/benchmarking-hub-update\"},{\"title\":\"Epoch overhauls its AI Benchmarking Hub to improve AI model evaluation - CO/AI\",\"url\":\"https://getcoai.com/news/epoch-overhauls-its-ai-benchmarking-hub-to-improve-ai-model-evaluation/\"},{\"title\":\"Epoch AI launches new benchmarking hub to verify AI model claims - CO/AI\",\"url\":\"https://getcoai.com/news/epoch-ai-launches-new-benchmarking-hub-to-verify-ai-model-claims/\"},{\"title\":\"About | Benchmarking | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/benchmarks/about\"},{\"title\":\"Data | Benchmarking | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/benchmarks/use-this-data\"},{\"title\":\"Data on AI Capabilities and Benchmarking | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/benchmarks\"},{\"title\":\"ECI Documentation – Included benchmarks | Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epoch.ai/data/eci-documentation/data\"},{\"title\":\"Benchmarking Hub update - Epoch AI\",\"url\":\"https://epochai.substack.com/p/benchmarking-hub-update\"}]\n\nI've found comprehensive information about Epoch AI's benchmarking hub methodology and data transparency approach. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Overview\n\nEpoch's AI Benchmarking Hub brings together results from many of the most informative AI benchmarks into one consistent, searchable place.\n\n## Methodology & Transparency\n\nEpoch AI overhauled its AI benchmarking infrastructure to provide more transparent, systematic, and up-to-date evaluations of AI model capabilities. The platform has several key methodological features:\n\n- **Systematic Coverage**: Epoch runs benchmarks across a wide range of models, covering recent as well as older models, small models and large models, and tracks both open and proprietary models.\n\n- **Current Updates**: Results for new models can be added to the database quickly after they are released (in many cases, results will be added the day a model is released).\n\n- **Evaluation Documentation**: The platform provides complete documentation of prompts, AI responses, and scoring for each evaluation question.\n\n## Downloadable Data & Accessibility\n\nEpoch provides downloadable data and detailed metadata for all benchmark results, enabling users to conduct their own analyses. Multiple access methods are available:\n\n- Users can download the data in CSV format, explore the data using interactive tools, or view the data directly in a table format.\n\n- Users can also use the Epoch AI Python client library (pip install epochai) to access the data via the Airtable API, which fully preserves relationships between entities, unlike the CSV download.\n\n## Licensing & Open Access\n\nData on the platform is publicly accessible under a permissive license, allowing other members of the research community to use it for their own analyses. Specifically, Epoch AI's data is free to use, distribute, and reproduce provided the source and authors are credited under the Creative Commons Attribution license.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"21975a1f-5373-4386-8a12-2b0ea06b6a6b","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:00.917Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6a9b0ce3-1b93-477b-809b-abc5b544739b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

DESK Magazine\",\"url\":\"https://vanschneider.com/blog/portfolio-tips/write-project-case-studies-portfolio/\"},{\"title\":\"Typography for Portfolio Design: Make Your Work Speak Before They Read | The Crit\",\"url\":\"https://thecrit.co/resources/typography-for-portfolios\"},{\"title\":\"All About Process: Dissecting Case Study Portfolios | Toptal®\",\"url\":\"https://www.toptal.com/designers/ui/case-study-portfolio\"},{\"title\":\"15 Best Case Study Design Examples that STAND OUT!\",\"url\":\"https://www.contentbeta.com/blog/case-study-design-examples/\"},{\"title\":\"Case Study: Gianluca Gradogna — Portfolio ’25 | Codrops\",\"url\":\"https://tympanus.net/codrops/2025/01/30/case-study-gianluca-gradogna-portfolio-25/\"},{\"title\":\"Case Study Portfolio Tips for Success in 2025\",\"url\":\"https://www.artfolio.com/article/structuring-case-studies-inside-your-portfolio-to-solve-real-client-pain-points\"},{\"title\":\"Portfolio Case Study Examples: Complete Guide |...\",\"url\":\"https://influenceflow.io/resources/portfolio-case-study-examples-complete-guide-with-real-world-samples/\"},{\"title\":\"10 tips to organize your design portfolio | by Slava Shestopalov | Design Bridges | Medium\",\"url\":\"https://medium.com/design-bridges/design-portfolio-88322e124791\"}]\n\nI found several resources about designing case study pages for portfolios in 2025. Here are the key findings:\n\n## Figure Captions and Visual Context\n\nNo visual should appear without an accompanying caption, heading, or explanation. If someone scrolls through your case study and only reads the little 1-2 sentence captions, they should still understand your project.\n\nCaptions under each frame should articulate the micro-decision it represents, making the business relevance explicit at a glance.\n\n## Page Layout and Navigation\n\nOne important guideline is to choose a layout that will enable you to include copy and image captions throughout. Portfolio content includes case study narratives, project descriptions, process explanations, image captions, and navigation labels.\n\n## Editorial Approach\n\nFrame each section around the challenges you faced, how you approached them, what options you considered, and what trade-offs you made. For visual presentation, use scannable sections, short captions under visuals, and one clear metric per result.\n\n## Interactive Elements and Navigation Features\n\nUsers have commented on how seamless and immersive the website feels, especially with the infinite scroll and the horizontal navigation for the photography section. Consider mini-timelines with horizontal scroll of milestones.\n\nThe search results emphasize balancing visual and textual content, using clear navigation, and ensuring every image has meaningful context through captions.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"715e47d1-1a75-4749-924c-59dd879d4e56","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:02.750Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b123e3c8-0832-423a-a6cc-ef7594cf0ace","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"6bfb9cde-2b5e-4e52-856d-9ce82baad90e","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KPEPkZ5uw8fAyMMZ4P68zQ","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"gwern.net design philosophy sidenotes visited links reading state\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Design Of This Website · Gwern.net\",\"url\":\"https://gwern.net/design\"},{\"title\":\"“Manual of Style”, by Gwern, GPT-5 Pro, Claude-4.7-opus · Gwern.net\",\"url\":\"https://gwern.net/style-guide\"},{\"title\":\"Sidenotes In Web Design · Gwern.net\",\"url\":\"https://gwern.net/sidenote\"},{\"title\":\"Design Graveyard · Gwern.net\",\"url\":\"https://gwern.net/design-graveyard\"},{\"title\":\"index | gwern.net unofficial docs\",\"url\":\"https://gwern.pleometric.net/\"},{\"title\":\"Gwern Branwen | Longterm Wiki\",\"url\":\"https://www.longtermwiki.com/wiki/E574\"},{\"title\":\"GitHub - gwern/gwern.net: Site infrastructure for gwern.net. Custom Hakyll website with unique link archiving, popup UX, transclusions/collapses, dark+reader mode, bidirectional backlinks, and typography (sidenotes, dropcaps, link icons, inflation-adjustment, subscripted-citations). · GitHub\",\"url\":\"https://github.com/gwern/gwern.net\"},{\"title\":\"About This Website · Gwern.net\",\"url\":\"https://gwern.net/about\"},{\"title\":\"Essays · Gwern.net\",\"url\":\"https://gwern.net/index\"},{\"title\":\"After seeing Gwern's website[0], I immediately knew what design I wanted for my ... | Hacker News\",\"url\":\"https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=19607589\"}]\n\nI found comprehensive information about gwern.net's design philosophy. Here are the key points:\n\n## Design Philosophy Overview\n\nGwern.net features unusual elements including monochrome aesthetics, sidenotes instead of footnotes on wide windows, efficient dropcaps, smallcaps, collapsible sections, automatic inflation-adjusted currency, Wikipedia-style link icons & infoboxes, custom syntax highlighting, extensive local archives to fight linkrot, and an ecosystem of \"popup\"/\"popover\" annotations & previews of links for frictionless browsing.\n\n## Sidenotes\n\nIn typography/design, sidenotes place footnotes/endnotes in the margins for easier reading, and they are particularly useful for web pages, where 'footnotes' are de facto endnotes, and clicking back and forth to endnotes is a pain for readers. Footnotes/Sidenotes are the same thing, chosen based on responsive design.\n\n## Information Hierarchy & Reading State\n\nA Gwern.net page can be read at multiple structural levels, high to low: title, metadata block, abstracts, section headers, margin notes, emphasized keywords in list items, footnotes/sidenotes, collapsed sections or paragraphs, internal cross-referencing links to other sections (such as appendices) which popup for immediate reading, and fulltext links or internal links to other pages (also popping up).\n\n## Link Handling\n\nMany of the reading features are custom infrastructure: hover popups for citations and local pages, inline transclusion, margin sidenotes, generated bibliographies, annotation databases, and link archiving.\n\n## Visual & Typographic Features\n\nDesign principles include monochrome aesthetics, sidenotes on wide screens, dropcaps and smallcaps, collapsible sections, inflation-adjusted currency displays, Wikipedia-style infoboxes, custom syntax highlighting, multi-column lists, and advanced poetry support.\n\nThe overall philosophy emphasizes treating web pages as extended reading environments that support deep exploration and non-linear reading patterns.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"5f4ba7ea-56b3-44bc-8047-ca94aa7fe394","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:04.520Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6bfb9cde-2b5e-4e52-856d-9ce82baad90e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

Let me run some targeted searches and fetch a few references to ground these ideas.\n\nI'm pulling from Tufte CSS for its apparatus conventions, Gwern's design for metadata and link state patterns, Epoch AI's benchmark methodology, and a recent Codrops case study on portfolio structure. That's seven parallel requests to gather concrete {"parentUuid":"19e1ab37-c341-4ca2-ac4b-f333a3a52136","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018gzZAxoxHPPt9vFF4zb3yS","type":"tool_result","content":"# Tufte CSS Implementation Apparatus\n\n## Sidenotes & Margin Notes\nSidenotes use paired HTML elements: an inline `<label>` with `sidenote-number` class and a hidden checkbox toggle, followed by a `<span class=\"sidenote\">` containing content. On large screens, these display in the margin; on small screens, users toggle visibility via the reference number. Margin notes omit the numbering, using a symbol (⊕) instead, reducing visual distraction while increasing cognitive matching effort.\n\n## Figure Conventions\n- **Main-column figures**: Default `<figure>` elements constrained to text width\n- **Margin figures**: Images wrapped in margin notes within `<p>` tags\n- **Full-width figures**: Apply the `fullwidth` class for screen-spanning displays\n- **Responsive iframes**: Use `iframe-wrapper` class for YouTube embeds and embedded content\n\nThe philosophy emphasizes \"tight integration of graphics with text\" without forcing users to navigate away from content.\n\n## Typography & Text Styling\nTufte CSS employs ETBook font (with separate files for bold and italic rather than mechanical browser transformation) with graceful fallbacks to Palatino and Georgia. The palette uses near-white (#fffff8) and near-black (#111111) to reduce harsh contrast. Links remain body-text color with underlines only—no color-change on interaction—preserving readability while maintaining discoverability.\n\n## Notable Omissions\nThe document discusses no table styling, only mentioning epigraphs as specialized `blockquote` elements with italicized quotes and `<footer>` source attribution.\n\n## For Evidence-Heavy Case Studies\nPrioritize margin notes for citations and asides, employ full-width figures for complex data visualizations, maintain restrained color use, and adopt the hierarchical heading limit (h1-h3 only) to encourage clear information architecture."}]},"uuid":"1e55e7f3-b4e1-49ac-8b41-62581633b843","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:45.044Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"19e1ab37-c341-4ca2-ac4b-f333a3a52136","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"9f1e2fe9-a341-428e-9eb9-9702839791d6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BPDvFAmQcjVkMTYtdpSLbn","type":"tool_result","content":"# How Epoch AI Presents Benchmark Evidence Credibly\n\nEpoch AI employs several concrete conventions to establish credibility:\n\n## Methodology Disclosure\n- Public task definitions published on GitHub gists for internally-run benchmarks\n- Explicit documentation of \"consistent and well documented settings across models\"\n- Detailed FAQ explaining prompting strategies, temperature settings, and model variants used\n\n## Per-Result Provenance\n- **Dates & Versions**: Models identified specifically (e.g., \"Llama 3.1 405B Instruct, not base model\")\n- **Error Bars**: \"Confidence interval of plus and minus one standard error\" shown in visualizations, with most models run 16 times per benchmark\n- **Source Attribution**: External results cite \"official leaderboards or primary sources\" with dedicated Source and Source link columns\n\n## Data Licensing\n- Results released under \"Creative Commons Attribution license\" with required credit\n- External data retains original licenses (Apache 2.0 for Aider, Terminal-Bench data)\n- Clear statement: users must \"ensure compliance with the respective license terms\"\n\n## Downloadable Formats\n- CSV download available\n- Python client library accessing Airtable API preserving data relationships\n- Interactive table exploration via Airtable embeds\n\n## Self-Run vs. Reported Distinction\n- Separate bases: \"Epoch AI internal runs\" versus \"External runs\"\n- Transparency note: \"we did not run the evaluations ourselves\" for external benchmarks\n- Caveat on external data: \"we do not have as detailed insight into the results\""}]},"uuid":"e16649d1-a96d-4123-b53b-51a8c4d8a023","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:45.091Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9f1e2fe9-a341-428e-9eb9-9702839791d6","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

{"parentUuid":"05e51c4b-6df3-44d7-ad8d-1c7e785642ec","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_014FGa8Yu8A6rU8LBJCsxdWa","type":"tool_result","content":"# Portfolio Structure Analysis\n\nBased on the case study content, here's how Gianluca Gradogna's portfolio is organized:\n\n## Meta Information & Project Details\nThe case study mentions a **tech stack** section listing:\n- Framework: Nuxt.js\n- Animations: GSAP\n- Scroll: Custom Smooth Scroll\n\nCredits identify the designer and developer roles separately.\n\n## Typography & Layout\nThe designer employed a \"clean, sans-serif font\" for design sections paired with refined typefaces for photography work. The approach reflects \"Swiss approach to typography\" principles, maintaining visual cohesion while differentiating content areas.\n\n## Visual Presentation\nImages are presented within an \"infinite scroll homepage\" structure, with a dedicated \"horizontal scroll page\" for travel photography. The layout emphasizes immersive exploration rather than traditional linear navigation.\n\n## Interaction & Transitions\nThe portfolio prioritizes smooth transitions using three core animation parameters: \"Position, Opacity, and Clip Masks.\" Gabriel Norman developed a custom scrolling system synchronizing looping animations across sections.\n\n## Navigation Structure\nWhile specific prev/next navigation details aren't described, the infinite scroll and horizontal scroll mechanics suggest fluid movement between projects.\n\n**Portfolio URL:** [gianlucagradogna.com](https://gianlucagradogna.com/)"}]},"uuid":"66dd4c8d-9a28-409a-a96d-fea2979287ec","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:47.978Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"05e51c4b-6df3-44d7-ad8d-1c7e785642ec","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"2eab8097-921f-44d9-a0ae-637bc134cb62","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01M5dSs8yZfqAUjVkH3cZ2ji","type":"tool_result","content":"# Gwern.net Design Conventions\n\n## (1) Page Metadata Block\n\nThe site uses a YAML front-matter convention displaying:\n- **Standard metadata**: title, description, author, creation/modification dates\n- **Unusual metadata**: status (notes/in-progress/finished), epistemic confidence level, importance rating (0-10)\n- **Bibliographic tools**: backlinks section and annotated link bibliography\n\nExample indicators shown: `status: finished`, `confidence: highly likely`, `importance: 3`\n\n## (2) Visited Links & Reading State\n\nThe document does not explicitly detail visited link styling. However, it mentions a **\"demo-mode\"** feature that \"tracks use-count of site features\" via LocalStorage, automatically disabling obtrusive elements after repeated exposure. This allows the site to simplify itself for returning readers without server integration.\n\n## (3) Sidenotes\n\nSidenotes replace footnotes on wide windows, positioned in left/right margins near their annotations. The implementation uses a custom library (`sidenotes.js`) that switches to floating footnotes on narrow screens. The design references historical precedents like the Geneva Bible and Pierre Bayle's Dictionary.\n\n## (4) Link Icons & Annotations/Popups\n\n**Link icons** encode filetype, domain, topic, author, or logo—hundreds of variations exist. A fraktur 'G' (𝔊) indicates internal Gwern.net essays; arrows (↑↓) show cross-references within/between pages.\n\n**Three link types**:\n- Solid underline: ordinary link with tooltip\n- Solid with left-hook ('|_'): live-link showing page preview on hover\n- Dotted underline: annotated link popping mini-page with metadata, abstract, and recursive capability\n\nPopups are draggable, resizable, pinnable, and fullscreen-capable.\n\n## (5) Tables of Contents & Evidence Presentation\n\nThe site employs **hierarchical structural reading**:\n- Title → metadata → abstract → section headers → margin notes → body text → collapsed regions → internal links → external links\n\n**Margin notes** briefly summarize paragraphs, appearing in left margin or inline on mobile. Collapsed regions hide content by default, revealed via hover/click. **Backlinks** display context snippets showing where pages are referenced elsewhere, often inlined within relevant sections.\n\nTags create hierarchical browsing with transcluded content, sortable by semantic similarity using embeddings.\n\n## (6) Typography Choices\n\n- **Palette**: deliberate grayscale for consistency and focus\n- **Tools**: dropcaps, small caps for emphasis; classic typographic details\n- **Font**: Source Serif/Sans (Adobe); IBM Plex Mono for code (chosen for variant zeroes support)\n- **Syntax highlighting**: custom ALGOL-inspired monochrome theme\n- **Math**: LaTeX compiled to HTML/CSS/Unicode when possible for efficiency\n- **Spacing**: semantic linefeeds (\"one sentence per line\") in source files\n- **Inflation adjustment**: automatic real-value calculation for historical dollar amounts (e.g., \"$5 in 1980\")"}]},"uuid":"6ce7914a-7279-4033-98e5-446a2de9f75b","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:09:49.749Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"2eab8097-921f-44d9-a0ae-637bc134cb62","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

The key insight from HF is how they render structured evaluation metadata as interactive widgets and tie it to their verified leaderboard system.\n\nFor changelogs, keepachangelog.com is the standard reference, though Linear's changelog design—with dated entries, images, and concise titles—represents the polished approach I should study. I'll rely on what I know but might do a quick fetch to confirm the presentation conventions.\n\nNow I'm exploring how to handle visit memory—tracking which essays a user has already read. There are several design precedents worth considering: the classic web pattern of visited links turning purple, sites like Hacker News graying out read titles, or feed readers showing unread indicators. There's also a physical metaphor angle with library due-date cards that could match something like an \"opened jul 18\" timestamp. I'm searching for existing essays or design patterns around this—maybe from A List Apart, jsomers, or Robin Rendle—and thinking about how to implement scroll position memory and reading progress for returning visitors.\n\nI'm also noticing that video game save files and \"continue where you left off\" functionality could be a strong precedent here. And for the figure/plate aesthetic specifically, the Public Domain Review is a perfect reference—they present historical plates from old books with source attributions, which directly matches the tipped-in plate convention I'm aiming for.\n\nLooking at contemporary examples: Works in Progress magazine handles footnotes and figures well, and Asterisk magazine is doing beautiful Tufte-style sidenotes in a modern, evidence-forward way. For tasteful deep links to specific file commits, Simon Willison's TILs and GitHub's permalink convention (the y key) are solid precedents, plus Observable's downloadable CSV pattern. Now I'm checking Codrops and a few other sources to see how they handle these conventions in practice. Maybe I should fetch a specific article from Asterisk to see their design patterns in action.
