{"parentUuid":"962a717e-ab55-4073-be06-542a9118d114","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012Xjp6mTmpBwcNehxb4rT5F","type":"tool_result","content":"# MLPerf Benchmark Results Presentation\n\n## Divisions\n\nMLPerf offers two divisions reflecting different reimplementation flexibility:\n\n- **Closed Division**: Compares platforms \"apples-to-apples\" by \"requiring using the same model as the reference implementation\"\n- **Open Division**: \"intended to foster innovation\" and permits alternative models or retraining\n\n## Availability Categories\n\nResults are categorized by component availability:\n\n- **Available**: \"only components that are available for purchase or for rent in the cloud\"\n- **Preview**: Systems expected to become Available in the next submission round\n- **RDI**: \"experimental, in development, or internal-use hardware or software\"\n\n## Results Table Structure\n\nEach submission row contains standardized information:\n\n- Submitter organization\n- Software framework and hardware library details\n- System description\n- Benchmark results for each test\n- Processor type and quantity\n- Accelerator type and quantity\n- Links to submission metadata and code\n\nOpen Division rows additionally include model identification and submitter notes.\n\n## Key Presentation Conventions\n\n- Each row represents \"a set of results produced by a single submitter using the same software stack and hardware platform\"\n- \"Published results are sometimes modified or invalidated for various reasons\" with changes documented in a public change log\n- Power measurement columns appear when applicable, showing system power or energy per stream\n- Interactive filtering and expandable columns enable detailed exploration"}]},"uuid":"d43d4463-3225-4629-a7f8-b9318122dc44","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:10:52.177Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"962a717e-ab55-4073-be06-542a9118d114","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"6b8794ac-ac5d-41ba-8eac-251f1b2fa96c","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01VUJRBjh1uMESzrXeUtPMU4","type":"tool_result","content":"# Editorial Design Conventions: Bisous Production Portfolio\n\n**Site URL:** https://bisous-production.com/\n\n## Typography & Grid System\n\nThe design employs a dual-typeface approach: a \"clean, refined\" sans-serif for luxury branding aesthetic, paired with monospaced type referencing \"production workflows and CGI processes.\" The entire experience is \"structured around four main columns,\" providing \"balance and precision\" while allowing \"large-scale imagery, cinematic transitions, and editorial compositions to breathe naturally.\"\n\n## Color & Minimalist Approach\n\nThe \"color palette intentionally remains restrained and minimal,\" letting \"imagery and motion define the emotional tone.\" This editorial restraint prioritizes visual content over decorative elements.\n\n## Project Navigation & Structure\n\nNavigation between works uses \"a secondary navigation system inspired by technical user interfaces,\" reimagined through minimalist principles. Projects feature a \"continuous transition system that seamlessly leads users into the next work,\" maintaining immersion rather than creating \"abrupt navigation breaks.\"\n\n## Micro-Interactions\n\nTitle animations employ \"randomized opacity transitions occurring letter by letter,\" remaining \"intentionally restrained yet organic.\" Hidden easter eggs feature \"letters extracted from the word 'Bisous'\" embedded in hover states and menu interactions.\n\n## Overall Apparatus\n\nRather than traditional print conventions, the design adopts a \"full-screen vertical slider\" with \"infinite scrolling,\" establishing a cinematic rather than editorial framework."}]},"uuid":"44d2a541-a6b5-4df2-b917-b0d41145bfd9","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:10:52.611Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"6b8794ac-ac5d-41ba-8eac-251f1b2fa96c","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"bc587d3c-b4d9-4afd-9343-db7d820c81d9","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017jTyqRb2jH2n1TyWZkwNbE","type":"tool_result","content":"# Project Page Collection - Websites & Patterns\n\n## Websites Listed\n\nThe collection showcases 173 items featuring project pages from various studios and designers:\n\n- **Immersive Garden** - immersivegarden.com\n- **Thomas Monavon** - thomasmonavon.com\n- **Bruno Arizio** - brunoarizio.com\n- **Ciel Rose** - cielrose.tv\n- **RXK Studio** - rxkstudio.com\n- **Oscar Pico** - oscarpico.es\n- **BASEBORN** - baseborn.studio\n- **Inkfish** - inkfishnyc.com\n- **Malvah** - malvah.co\n- **Ask Phill** - askphill.com\n- **Tux Creative House** - tuxkarma.co\n- **AVAA Architects** - avaa-architectes.fr\n- **Thoughtlab** - thoughtlab.com\n- **Plastic Bionic** - plasticbionic.com\n\n## Common Presentation Patterns\n\nThe collection demonstrates these recurring design approaches:\n\n1. **Interactive Elements** - Scroll effects, hover interactions, and transitions dominate the featured examples\n\n2. **Layout Variations** - Designers employ \"fullscreen, grid and list\" views and horizontal scrolling mechanics\n\n3. **Navigation Strategies** - Project menus use overlays, bubble interfaces, and directional navigation\n\n4. **Visual Hierarchy** - Headers, galleries, and content-focused layouts guide user attention through work showcases\n\n5. **Animation Integration** - Most examples incorporate movement through CSS, GSAP, or similar technologies to enhance engagement"}]},"uuid":"65644aa9-6b4c-43e6-a7a3-062baed37ad4","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:10:53.236Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"bc587d3c-b4d9-4afd-9343-db7d820c81d9","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"f1c6fa51-0bd6-4d1c-9de0-4a369e8e0587","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KjV3APvrJa12EcBYyK6en2","type":"tool_result","content":"# Stefan Vitasović Portfolio — Project Page Structure\n\n**Portfolio URL:** https://stefanvitasovic.dev/\n\n## Project Display Architecture\n\nThe case study doesn't explicitly detail the project meta layout (client, year, role, stack presentation). However, it emphasizes that the site is \"aimed at showcasing work focused on motion and interactivity\" with a minimalist aesthetic.\n\n## Visual Presentation\n\nProjects feature **video rendering through WebGL**, with high-definition content hosted on Cloudflare R2. Videos are implemented as textures within the WebGL layer, and the design applies consistent shader effects across all media. On mobile, native HTML5 video elements replace the WebGL implementation.\n\n## Design Grid & Typography\n\nThe layout employs \"offset grid layouts\" creating asymmetric, unbalanced compositions. A key typographic motif involves character-to-word animations where \"the words are divided into individual characters which then come together through motion.\"\n\n## Page Transitions\n\nNavigation between projects uses \"custom transitions between pages that aim to feel natural and engaging,\" powered by Framer Motion's **AnimatePresence** component. These feature dynamic fade effects orchestrated through a shared Layout component.\n\n## Navigation\n\nThe projects page functions as an overview enabling \"quick way back to the splash screen by scrolling upwards.\" However, the specific prev/next project navigation mechanism isn't detailed in this case study."}]},"uuid":"beddc8ad-ad96-482f-b2ec-c524598d687b","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:10:55.055Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f1c6fa51-0bd6-4d1c-9de0-4a369e8e0587","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

I found relevant information about visited link indicator design. The search results show several examples of subtle checkmark designs for visited links. Let me provide you with the findings:\n\nBased on the search results, I found information about subtle \"visited link\" indicator design patterns, including checkmark designs, which appear to be a design pattern that several designers and developers have explored:\n\nJoel Califa designed clear checkmarks with the word 'Seen' next to every link a user has been to, implementing a pseudo-element with a checkmark and the word 'Seen'.\n\nSimilarly, Mike Industries experimented with a checkmark using the mathematical \"radical\" sign (√) which looks like a hand sketched checkmark at small sizes.\n\nIn a list of blog posts, visited posts could show a \"visited\" label styled with a border to prominently announce to users that a blog post has been visited in the past.\n\nThese designs address the challenge that many users read blogs in their browsers and benefit from visual indicators to know which posts they have or haven't read.\n\nHowever, I should note that while my search found information about visited link indicator design patterns in general, I didn't find specific references to essays or blog posts by Gwern or Maggie Appleton specifically discussing or implementing this particular pattern. {"parentUuid":"8345f2f6-f7c0-4858-8160-33c26893b46d","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5","id":"msg_011Cd9f7stSwbE35CZjFaiTd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01HThKJTE3f4kNe3iKwQKwK5","name":"WebFetch","input":{"url":"https://rauno.me/craft","prompt":"Describe how this personal craft/portfolio page presents work: entry structure, dates, numbering, captions for each experiment/prototype, typography, and what makes it feel like documented evidence of craft rather than a marketing portfolio."},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9663,"cache_read_input_tokens":61237,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9663,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Cd9f7pKe63xjxHnqRZTUd","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"109028ea-f481-428a-aed3-919a83eeda56","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:46.804Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"dd4ce6aa-297c-4a97-90f4-dacfd148651a","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01NsQRJLP9xaA4Xed9RXiahq","type":"tool_result","content":"# Visited Link Design Pattern Summary\n\n## Visual Implementation\nThe author marks visited links with a checkmark icon and the label \"Seen\" appended to each link. This approach creates immediate, unambiguous feedback about navigation history.\n\n## Technical Reasoning\nBrowser security restrictions severely limit `:visited` styling capabilities. As noted: \"We can't apply CSS to the link's child elements. We can't add extra pseudo-elements.\" This privacy protection prevents history-sniffing but eliminates creative solutions for visited states.\n\n## localStorage Solution\nThe implementation stores visited page pathnames in browser storage, then iterates through all internal links to check against this data. When a match is found, a `data-visited` attribute is added, enabling full CSS customization—including pseudo-elements and content insertion that `:visited` alone cannot achieve.\n\n## Elegance Through Subtlety\nThe design succeeds by maintaining restraint. Rather than creating visual noise, the author recommends darkening existing link colors to mirror the traditional blue-to-purple convention. For custom color schemes: \"make them darker\" to preserve the tacit understanding users already have about visited link semantics.\n\nThe key insight is that small investment yields substantial returns—reducing user errors and frustration while respecting both aesthetic goals and usability standards."}]},"uuid":"a472284d-58f3-4a5f-be12-d507c51a6ddd","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:47.981Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"dd4ce6aa-297c-4a97-90f4-dacfd148651a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"a472284d-58f3-4a5f-be12-d507c51a6ddd","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5","id":"msg_011Cd9f7stSwbE35CZjFaiTd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01By7xxFA5aVZUgPCduDMZyF","name":"WebFetch","input":{"url":"https://linear.app/changelog","prompt":"Describe how this changelog presents entries: dating convention, entry structure (title, prose, images), visual restraint, permalinks, and what makes it a design benchmark for changelogs."},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9663,"cache_read_input_tokens":61237,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9663,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Cd9f7pKe63xjxHnqRZTUd","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"9d1b3765-57e9-4a5a-9ad5-b530d7852ca3","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:48.163Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"9d1b3765-57e9-4a5a-9ad5-b530d7852ca3","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5","id":"msg_011Cd9f7stSwbE35CZjFaiTd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_012eRnf4ABJYFuQTthjE1GTK","name":"WebSearch","input":{"query":"Public Domain Review essay image caption source line rights labels plates presentation"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9663,"cache_read_input_tokens":61237,"output_tokens":2547,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9663},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":2547,"cache_read_input_tokens":61237,"cache_creation_input_tokens":9663,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":9663,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Cd9f7pKe63xjxHnqRZTUd","attributionAgent":"general-purpose","type":"assistant","uuid":"d1c4b057-49bb-4a48-ba1d-647e1110d8af","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:48.414Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"8345f2f6-f7c0-4858-8160-33c26893b46d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Nm4aq25P2rtRMmSk6239t1","type":"tool_result","content":"# Distill Article Components and Conventions\n\n## Article Components Present\n\nBased on the documentation provided, Distill articles include:\n\n**Metadata & Byline:**\n- Front matter specifies title, description, authors with links, and affiliations\n- The `<dt-byline></dt-byline>` tag displays this information\n\n**Citations:**\n- Implemented via `<dt-cite key=\"identifier\"></dt-cite>` tags referencing a bibliography\n- \"Bibtex is the supported way of making academic citations\"\n- Displays as numbered references with hover information\n\n**Footnotes:**\n- Created using `<dt-fn>` tags\n- \"The number of the footnote will be automatically generated\"\n\n**Appendix:**\n- `<dt-appendix>` section follows the main article\n- Suggested sections include Acknowledgments and Author Contributions\n\n## Components NOT Documented\n\nThe provided guide does **not** address:\n- Figure numbering or captioning conventions (though layout classes for figures exist)\n- DOI or published date fields\n- Correction/update policies\n- Formal review documentation\n- Dedicated `d-figure` or `d-footnote` component specifications\n\nThe document focuses on technical implementation rather than editorial policies or complete metadata standards."}]},"uuid":"d551f517-832d-4618-95c7-04a0d82f5a08","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:49.315Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8345f2f6-f7c0-4858-8160-33c26893b46d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"109028ea-f481-428a-aed3-919a83eeda56","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HThKJTE3f4kNe3iKwQKwK5","type":"tool_result","content":"# Craft Portfolio Structure Analysis\n\nThis portfolio presents work as a chronological archive of interactive experiments and design implementations, structured as evidence of iterative craft rather than polished marketing.\n\n## Entry Architecture\n\nEach piece follows a consistent format:\n- **Date stamp** (month/year), positioned to establish temporal context\n- **Title** describing the specific interaction or component\n- **Thumbnail image** or visual preview\n- **Optional action link** (\"View Production,\" \"View Prototype,\" or \"Read Essay\")\n\nThe reverse chronological ordering—from February 2026 back to April 2021—frames this as an active documentation of recent explorations.\n\n## Typographic Choices\n\nThe presentation uses minimal hierarchy. Titles appear in regular weight, dates in smaller caps. This understated treatment avoids promotional flourish, instead suggesting technical documentation or lab notes.\n\n## Evidence of Craft\n\nSeveral structural decisions reinforce authenticity:\n\n**Variety in medium**: The archive mixes prototypes, production work, essays, and shader experiments. This heterogeneity signals genuine exploration rather than a curated \"best of\" collection.\n\n**Specificity of description**: Rather than vague claims, titles identify precise interactions: \"Exclusion Tabs,\" \"Wheel Input,\" \"Gooey Shader.\" This technical vocabulary suggests hands-on implementation.\n\n**Visible iteration**: Multiple related pieces (\"Microinteractions I & II,\" \"Precision & Fractional Slider\") demonstrate recursive refinement.\n\n**Unglamorous presentation**: Low-contrast base64 image placeholders and minimal visual polish emphasize substance over design salesmanship."}]},"uuid":"d4b1d57d-ccb4-44fc-a4c0-b5b25c421ba5","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:54.105Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"109028ea-f481-428a-aed3-919a83eeda56","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"d1c4b057-49bb-4a48-ba1d-647e1110d8af","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012eRnf4ABJYFuQTthjE1GTK","type":"tool_result","content":"Web search results for query: \"Public Domain Review essay image caption source line rights labels plates presentation\"\n\nLinks: [{\"title\":\"Reusing Material — The Public Domain Review\",\"url\":\"https://publicdomainreview.org/reusing-material/\"},{\"title\":\"Rights Labelling on Our Site | Public Domain Image Archive\",\"url\":\"https://pdimagearchive.org/rights-labelling-on-our-site/\"},{\"title\":\"The Art Bulletin | Publications | CAA\",\"url\":\"https://www.collegeart.org/publications/art-bulletin/preparation\"},{\"title\":\"Rights Labelling on Our Site — The Public Domain Review\",\"url\":\"https://publicdomainreview.org/rights-labelling-on-our-site/\"},{\"title\":\"Referencing Images - Media & Communications - LibGuides at Edith Cowan University\",\"url\":\"https://ecu.au.libguides.com/media-comms/ref\"},{\"title\":\"EXSCLAIM! -- An automated pipeline for the construction of labeled   materials imaging datasets from literature\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2103.10631\"},{\"title\":\"Public Domain 12M: A Highly Aesthetic Image-Text Dataset with Novel   Governance Mechanisms\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.23144\"},{\"title\":\"Image Credits and Captions - How to Cite Your Sources - Research Guides at Carleton College\",\"url\":\"https://gouldguides.carleton.edu/citation/imagecaptions\"},{\"title\":\"A Checklist to Publish Collections as Data in GLAM Institutions\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2304.02603\"},{\"title\":\"Is a Picture Worth Ten Thousand Words in a Review Dataset?\",\"url\":\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/1606.07496\"}]\n\nBased on the search results, I found relevant information about the Public Domain Review's approach to image captions and rights labeling:\n\nThe historical material featured in the Public Domain Review's Essays series is not accompanied by rights labels, just links to the original source. Images featured on the site are often edited by the team to improve their aesthetic presentation. However, the Public Domain Review asserts no additional rights to these edited versions.\n\nRegarding image reuse, if reusing material for commercial purposes from either the Collections or Essays section, it is recommended to further investigate any usage restrictions by clicking through to the source using the provided links. The Public Domain Review does not assert rights relating to any of the historical images featured on the site and is not in a position to grant permissions for use—interested parties should contact the original source institution.\n\n\nREMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks."}]},"uuid":"3b77e647-54bb-4a5a-968b-616a9b6195cb","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:54.551Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"d1c4b057-49bb-4a48-ba1d-647e1110d8af","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}
{"parentUuid":"9d1b3765-57e9-4a5a-9ad5-b530d7852ca3","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"3f224581-549d-4589-82b7-499fa8d3285c","agentId":"abecf0e0209c51bae","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01By7xxFA5aVZUgPCduDMZyF","type":"tool_result","content":"# Linear's Changelog: A Design Benchmark\n\n## Dating Convention\nEntries follow reverse chronological order with explicit dates in `Month Day, Year` format (e.g., \"July 2, 2026\"), making it straightforward to locate recent updates.\n\n## Entry Structure\nEach changelog entry comprises:\n- **Headline**: Concise feature title\n- **Visual element**: Screenshots or embedded video players demonstrating functionality\n- **Descriptive prose**: Paragraph-length explanations grounding features in user benefit\n- **Organized sections**: Fixes, improvements, API changes, and keyboard shortcuts separated into collapsible or labeled categories\n\n## Visual Restraint\nThe design avoids clutter through disciplined typography and whitespace. As the content shows, sections like \"Fixes\" contain dozens of items presented as compact, scannable lists rather than lengthy paragraphs—\"Fixed issues with editing collapsible sections\" typifies this economy.\n\n## Permalinks\nEach major entry includes a direct link (e.g., `/changelog/2026-07-02-initiative-properties`), enabling users to share or bookmark specific releases without losing context.\n\n## Benchmark Qualities\nLinear's changelog excels because it:\n- **Prioritizes substance over marketing**: Technical depth appears alongside user-facing benefits\n- **Maintains consistency**: Identical structural patterns across months of entries build predictability\n- **Balances comprehensiveness**: Detailed fix logs coexist with highlight announcements, serving both engineers and general users\n- **Supports skimming**: Hierarchical organization lets readers extract value at any depth level"}]},"uuid":"d6332e4a-b068-486f-98f1-7381d700ed1c","timestamp":"2026-07-18T14:11:54.964Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"9d1b3765-57e9-4a5a-9ad5-b530d7852ca3","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0","sessionId":"eb92e506-555c-4aa1-beed-a35ce5fdca9a","version":"2.1.209","gitBranch":"redesign/daylight-study"}

For evidence presentation, I'm applying Tufte's figure grammar with margin notes and full-width options, plus PDR's source-line convention and distill's caption approach, with plate numbering coming next.\n\nFor validation, I'm building an appendix table following Epoch's format (source, self-run vs external, error bars, date) and MLPerf's visibility badges for availability categories, with per-row anchors so the homepage can deep-link to specific validation rows—each row gets a one-line method description and date. For decisions, I'm adopting the ADR (architecture decision records) pattern with numbered, dated entries and status tracking (accepted/superseded). The next section carries forward with a teaser line that threads toward the following page, plus two presentation approaches: tipped-in plates echoing PDR's photographic tradition, and paper-based memory systems like Califa's demo mode. Compiling the final report.\n\n# Dossier apparatus research — how the best present technical evidence (July 2026)\n\nRead first: DOSSIER-SPEC.md, EVIDENCE-MODEL.md, DECISION.md, docs/NO-LIST.md (note: NO-LIST lives at `/Users/ayush/Documents/Projects/Portfolio-2.0/docs/NO-LIST.md`, not in design-lab/). Everything below is mapped against those four.\n\n---\n\n## (a) Exemplars — one transferable idea each\n\n### 1 · Award-tier case/project pages 2025–26\n\n- **Bisous (Codrops, Jun 2026)** — https://bisous-production.com/ · writeup: https://tympanus.net/codrops/2026/06/29/inside-bisous-designing-an-editorial-experience-for-cinematic-cgi/ — **The idea: a mono \"technical-UI\" secondary nav + a continuous transition that flows one project into the next with no break.** Their prev/next isn't a footer link; the next work *arrives*. Maps directly to \"thread exits toward the next document\" (spec pt. 8).\n- **Stefan Vitasović portfolio (SOTD-tier, 2025)** — https://stefanvitasovic.dev/ · writeup: https://tympanus.net/codrops/2025/03/05/case-study-stefan-vitasovic-portfolio-2025/ — **The idea: Swiss-print offset grid — meta and figures sit asymmetrically against generous emptiness, and page transitions run through one shared layout component** so index→case feels like one paper being re-shuffled, not a route change. (Your broken home→case transition, scored ~20, is exactly what his shared-layout persistence solves.)\n- **Awwwards \"Project Page\" collection (173 curated project pages)** — https://www.awwwards.com/awwwards/collections/project-page/ — **The idea: use it as the browsing index; the 2025–26 pattern across it is meta-as-typography (no cards), full-bleed figures alternating with column figures, and directional prev/next.** Named standouts in it worth a Chrome tour: brunoarizio.com, thomasmonavon.com, immersivegarden.com.\n- **Rauno Freiberg, craft ledger** — https://rauno.me/craft — **The idea: a personal portfolio that reads as *lab documentation*: reverse-chron dated entries, precise technical titles (\"Exclusion Tabs,\" \"Gooey Shader\"), zero promotional voice, visible iteration (Microinteractions I & II).** This is the closest existing thing to your dossier's register — evidence of craft by specificity, not adjectives.\n\n### 2 · Paper-styled / scholarly web apparatus\n\n- **Tufte CSS** — https://edwardtufte.github.io/tufte-css/ — **The idea: the three-width figure grammar (margin figure / main-column figure / full-width figure) plus numbered sidenotes that collapse to tap-toggles on mobile.** Also: links stay ink-colored with underline only — no color-change on hover. Near-white `#fffff8`, near-black `#111` — softened contrast is doctrine, which independently confirms your archive-cream stock.\n- **Gwern.net design** — https://gwern.net/design (sidenotes deep-dive: https://gwern.net/sidenote) — **The idea: the page metadata block — `status / confidence / importance / created / modified` — as honest scholarly self-assessment rendered as apparatus.** Also steal: link icons encoding destination *type* (his 𝔊 for internal, arrows for cross-refs → your typed-artifact links: repo / CI / ledger / demo each get a mono glyph), and \"demo-mode\" (localStorage use-counts that quiet the UI for returning readers — precedent for \"the paper remembers\").\n- **Distill.pub component guide** — https://distill.pub/guide/ (living heir: https://transformer-circuits.pub/) — **The idea: apparatus as *components with automatic numbering*: `dt-byline` (authors/affiliation/date), `dt-cite` (hover bibliography), `dt-fn` (auto-numbered footnotes), `dt-appendix` (acknowledgments + contributions).** The dossier equivalents: kicker block, footnote registry, validation appendix — build them as components so numbering can never drift (their footnote-4 self-falsification bug was a hand-numbering failure mode).\n\n### 3 · Industry evidence & provenance\n\n- **Epoch AI Benchmarking Hub** — https://epoch.ai/benchmarks · methodology: https://epoch.ai/benchmarks/about · data: https://epoch.ai/benchmarks/use-this-data — **The idea: every number carries a `Source` + `Source link` column, self-run results are explicitly separated from externally-reported ones (\"we did not run the evaluations ourselves\"), runs disclose n (16 runs, ±1 SE), and the whole dataset is downloadable CSV under CC-BY.** This is the receipts-table gold standard and directly validates the EVIDENCE-MODEL rule that no cell may cite the portfolio itself.\n- **MLPerf results (MLCommons)** — https://mlcommons.org/benchmarks/inference-datacenter/ — **The idea: honesty taxonomies as first-class columns: Closed vs Open division (apples-to-apples vs modified) and Available / Preview / RDI availability categories — plus a *public change log* where modified or invalidated results are documented rather than deleted.** Your AVX-512 retraction line is exactly this discipline; formalize it.\n- **Hugging Face annotated model card** — https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/model-card-annotated — **The idea: evaluation is a mandated triplet — testing data, factors, metrics — and structured eval metadata is machine-parsed into a rendered widget.** Your proofManifest→rendered `/evidence` index is the same move: one source of truth, rendered twice (per-page captions + site index).\n- **Linear changelog** — https://linear.app/changelog — **The idea: dated permalink slugs per entry (`/changelog/2026-07-02-initiative-properties`) with identical entry anatomy for months on end.** Predictable anatomy is what makes evidence skimmable; validation rows deserve the same permalink treatment.\n\n### 4 · Documentation as artifact\n\n- **Epoch again + Keep a Changelog** — https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/ — **The idea: the artifact is downloadable *and* diff-linked: dated ISO entries, an Unreleased section, every version heading linking to the compare-view diff.** Your run-ledger JSON should be checked in, rendered as an HTML table, and offered as a raw download link — SVG demoted to thumbnail (per EVIDENCE-MODEL pt. 6).\n- **GitHub permalinks** — https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/working-with-files/using-files/getting-permanent-links-to-files — **The idea: `blob/<sha>/path#L12-L40` is the canonical file@commit deep-link; press `y` to pin.** Render as mono text (`jobtracker@a1b2c3d · src/eval/harness.ts:88`) — a text link, never a shields.io image badge (badge-wall = banned vibe; zero image badges anywhere).\n\n### 5 · Visit-memory / \"the paper remembers\"\n\n- **Joel Califa, \"Revisiting :Visited\"** — https://joelcalifa.com/blog/revisiting-visited/ — **The idea: `:visited` CSS is privacy-crippled (no pseudo-elements, no child styling), so store visited *pathnames in localStorage*, stamp `data-visited` on matching internal links, then style freely — his checkmark + \"Seen\" label.** His subtlety guidance: the classic convention is simply a *darker* link; add marks sparingly. Related lineage: Mike Davidson's 2004 √-mark experiment — https://mikeindustries.com/blog/archive/2004/06/radical-links · NN/g guidelines — https://www.nngroup.com/articles/guidelines-for-visualizing-links/\n- **Gwern's demo-mode** (in /design, above) — **The idea: memory that *subtracts* rather than adds — the site quiets returning-visitor chrome using localStorage counts.** Elevation for you: on a revisit, the case file's \"opened\" stamp is already inked and static — the paper remembers by *not* re-performing.\n\n---\n\n## (b) The 8-point dossier spec × precedent — confirm / refine\n\n| # | Spec point | Verdict | Precedent + refinement |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Stock** — flat archive cream, no day-arc, ink + stamp-orange only | **Confirm** | Tufte's `#fffff8/#111` softened-contrast doctrine; Epoch/Linear both run near-mono with one accent. Refinement: NO-LIST §D2 demands a static frame never pass for a Daylight clone — on the flat cream this burden shifts entirely to grain + thread + Fraunces, so keep grain *perceptible* on case pages. |\n| 2 | **Opening** — ¶ kicker \"case file 02/07 · filed 2026-02\", title, deck, thread enters | **Confirm + extend** | Distill's `dt-byline` and gwern's metadata block. Refinement: make the kicker a *two-date* line — `filed 2026-02 · last verified 2026-07` — and add one gwern-style status word (`status: shipped` / `in review`). \"Last verified\" is the single highest-credibility token a dossier can carry (Epoch's whole pitch is recency). |\n| 3 | **Meta as ledger** — mono dot-leader table, no pills | **Confirm + extend** | Gwern metadata block; MLPerf's standardized row anatomy. Refinement (recruiter addendum): add rows `live demo ↗` (liveUrl is currently never rendered — the found bug) and `repo @ <sha>` pinned per GitHub permalink convention. Dot-leaders are period-correct for a working paper; no precedent conflict. |\n| 4 | **Evidence as plates** — tipped-in figures, mono captions, source line | **Confirm** | Tufte's three figure widths gives plates a *size grammar* (margin plate / column plate / full-width plate) instead of one-size; Public Domain Review's convention — every image links to its original source, no rights clutter (https://publicdomainreview.org/reusing-material/) — is your caption source-line: it must terminate *off-page* (repo@commit or CI run), enforcing the EVIDENCE-MODEL no-self-citation rule at the caption level. |\n| 5 | **Validation as appendix table** (centerpiece) | **Confirm + extend** | Epoch's column set is the model: claim · method (1 line) · artifact (file@SHA / CI run / ledger) · date · visibility. Extensions with precedent: (i) per-row permalink anchors (Linear slugs) so homepage footnotes deep-link to `#v-automl-3`; (ii) MLPerf-style division tag on any demo-data run (`demo dataset` vs `production`) — the AutoML run ledger *must* wear this; (iii) an n-disclosure wherever a rate appears (`19/20`, `n=96` with class mix — Epoch's ±SE habit); (iv) a corrections line under the table (MLPerf public change log) housing the AVX-512-style retractions permanently. |\n| 6 | **Decisions as numbered clauses** — \"d1 —\", tradeoff as mono footnote | **Confirm + extend** | This is the ADR (Architecture Decision Record) convention in print clothing — numbered, dated, with status. Refinement: give each clause an ADR status word — `d3 — accepted · revisited 2026-01` or `superseded by d5` — and auto-number footnotes distill-style so tradeoff notes can't drift. |\n| 7 | **Stamps for status** | **Confirm with discipline** | MLPerf's Available/Preview/RDI proves status *taxonomies* read as rigor; niccolomiranda's postage stamp (INSPIRATION.md) proves the graphic register. Refinement: a stamp = a *category from a closed set* (`private repository — evidence verified`, `approved`, `in review`), never ad-hoc decoration; ≤2 stamped moments per page so the home page's APPROVED climax stays the loudest stamp on the site. |\n| 8 | **Close with continuity** — folio footer, thread exits toward next file | **Confirm** | Bisous's continuous next-work arrival; Vitasović's shared-layout transitions; recruiter fix folded in: the return link goes to `/#work` (the dead `/#projects` anchor bug), labeled \"back to the work\". Extension: next-file teaser shows *its* kicker line (\"case file 03/07 · jobtracker — filed 2025-11\") so the footer is itself a ledger row, and inks a small ✓ if already read (Califa). |\n\n**Elevation precedents:** tipped-in plates ← PDR's edited-plates-with-source-links practice; ±rotation is *yours* (no strong precedent — see warning 3). Paper-remembers ← Califa localStorage `data-visited` + gwern demo-mode subtraction.\n\n---\n\n## (c) Five \"steal this\" details\n\n1. **Epoch's provenance split, as one mono line under the receipts table:** `runs marked ⌂ executed by me on demo data · marked ci link to the public run · external numbers cite their source`. One sentence kills the \"who says?\" question for every row above it, and legitimizes self-graded results by disclosure (the PolicyBot 17/25 requirement).\n2. **MLPerf's public change-log of invalidated results → a permanent \"corrections\" block** at the appendix foot, set like an erratum slip: `erratum · 2026-03 — retracted \"AVX-512\" claim; measured path was AVX2. row v-mnist-2 amended.` Never delete a number; amend it in public. This is the single most trust-building apparatus a personal site can carry, and no portfolio on Awwwards has it.\n3. **Tufte's three-width figure grammar applied to plates:** margin plate (small proof, e.g. a stamp-sized CI screenshot), column plate (standard fig with mat + caption), full-width plate (architecture fig. 2). Caption anatomy fixed: `fig. 4 — approval queue during run no. 041` / second line, always a link off-page: `source: jobtracker @ a1b2c3d · ci run #118 ↗`. If a caption can't terminate off-page, the EVIDENCE-MODEL says relabel it description — the caption component should literally have no non-link source variant.\n4. **Protocol block as a 5-line mono \"method slip\" tipped in beside the metric it qualifies** (Tufte margin-note position on wide screens, collapsible below the number on mobile): `sample: 96 held-out msgs (61 app / 35 other) · split: tuned on 40, evaled on 96 disjoint · judge: exact-label match · pass: ≥0.95 · run: 2026-01-12 · repro: npm run eval:email -- --seed 7`. The reproduce command line is what elevates it from claim to protocol (Epoch publishes task definitions; you publish the command).\n5. **Califa's localStorage `data-visited` for the reading trail:** store pathname + first-opened timestamp; case page inks a one-time `opened · jul 18, 10:04` stamp (then stays static forever — gwern's subtraction principle); home rail + work rows get a small ink ✓ via `[data-visited]::after`. No \"Seen\" labels — the tick and a *slightly darker* ink (his own subtlety rule) are enough. Zero server, degrades to nothing.\n\n---\n\n## (d) Warnings — cliché & NO-LIST adjacency\n\n1. **Vintage-ephemera cosplay is the dossier's native failure mode.** Stamps + tipped-in plates + rotation + stitching + open-date stamps, all at once, tips from \"working paper\" into scrapbook/Wes-Anderson prop — the 2025–26 wave of \"archival\" portfolio templates (and niccolomiranda's newspaper) sits exactly there, and NO-LIST §D1 already bans newspaper framing. Budget the skeuomorphs: rotation on *at most* the hero plate of each page, thread-stitch on one corner of one plate, ≤2 stamps. Every apparatus item must carry information (a number, a date, a status from a closed set) — PDR and Tufte stay dignified because nothing is texture-only.\n2. **Visibility badges must not become pills.** Spec pt. 3 bans pills/cards, and colored rounded badges in the receipts table would re-import them through the back door. Render visibility as bracketed mono text — `[public]` `[private — verified on request]` — or a small typed glyph, hairlines only. Same rule kills shields.io/CI image badges anywhere on the site: a CI link is mono text with the run number, or it doesn't appear.\n3. **Don't let apparatus outrun honesty — no academic cosplay.** A portfolio with fake DOI lines, \"cite this page\" BibTeX boxes, or arXiv-style abstract blocks would *manufacture* authority the content hasn't earned — the same sin as the self-referential evidence cells, in typographic form. Distill and gwern earn their apparatus because every component is functional. Litmus: if an apparatus element would survive with lorem ipsum in it, cut it.\n4. **The \"opened\" stamp is one restrained beat, not a performance.** If it animates in with ink-spread on load it becomes a mini boot-screen moment (NO-LIST §A spirit) and re-performs on every visit like the banned entrance-wrapper vocabulary. First visit: it's simply *there* by the time the reader reaches it (or a ≤300ms settle). Revisits: static, already dried. And never phrase it as surveillance (\"we know you were here\") — it's the reader's own pencil mark, first-person voice: `you opened this file · jul 18`.\n5. **Flat archive cream sits closest to two graves at once:** the abandoned paper-ink newspaper theme (§D1 — so: Fraunces not Playfair, kiln-clay orange never pure red, no column rules) *and* the Daylight-clone test (§D2 — a static case-page screenshot must still show grain + thread + Fraunces, since the dossier forfeits the day-arc that normally carries differentiation). The spec's \"one stop older\" stock is right, but ship it with the contour texture genuinely visible, or the case pages become the generic cream-editorial look that half of 2026's minimal portfolios already wear.\n\nSources: [Bisous writeup](https://tympanus.net/codrops/2026/06/29/inside-bisous-designing-an-editorial-experience-for-cinematic-cgi/) · [Vitasović
